L1695: **Immunogenicity:** Treatment-emergent ADAs were detected in 3/103 (2.9%) subjects in the vial-and-syringe group, 1/105 (1.0%) subjects in the APFS group, and 0/105 (0%) subjects in the AI group. No apparent impact of ADA on PK was observed, and no injection site reactions were reported in ADA-positive subjects.
L1696: ## • **Study 1183**
L1697: **Study Type:** Phase 1 multiple dose allergen challenge study in adult subjects with mild atopic asthma
L1698: **Title:** Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-controlled, Parallel Design, Multiple Dose Study to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamics of AMG 157 in Subjects With Mild Atopic Asthma
L1699: ## **Objective:**
L1700: The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the late asthmatic response after an allergen inhalation challenge in subjects with mild atopic asthma who received multiple doses of tezepelumab.
L1701: Secondary and exploratory objectives included the following:
L1702: - To determine the PK profile of tezepelumab after multiple-dose administration in subjects with mild atopic asthma.
- To evaluate the safety, tolerability, and immunogenicity of multiple-dose administration of tezepelumab in subjects with mild atopic asthma.
- To evaluate the early asthmatic response after an allergen inhalation challenge in subjects with mild atopic asthma who received multiple doses of tezepelumab.
L1703: ## **Study Design:**
L1704: The study recruited nonsmoking adult subjects with mild atopic asthma on asthma therapy limited to short-acting beta agonists taken less than Q2W. Subjects were randomised 1:1 to receive 700 mg IV tezepelumab or placebo Q4W for a total of 3 doses. Subjects were challenged with nebulized methacholine and an inhaled allergen at specified time points throughout the study. Intensive PK sampling was conducted up to Day 169.
L1705: ## **PK Results:**
L1706: PK data were evaluable for 16 subjects who were treated with tezepelumab. The mean (standard deviation [SD]) Cmax for the first and third doses were 262 (73) µg/mL and 325 (81) µg/mL, respectively. The mean (SD) for the AUCtau for the first and third doses were 2730 (700) and 4420 (1250) day*µg/mL, respectively. Mean (SD) estimate of t1/2,z after the third dose was 28.0 (4.2) days. Mean accumulation ratios were 1.31 for Cmax and 1.64 for AUCtau. The PK results were consistent with the results from the previous multiple dose (Study 0390) and single dose (Study 0620) studies with respect to the 700 mg IV cohorts.
L1707: ## **Immunogenicity:**
L1708: None of the subjects (0/16) who received tezepelumab tested positive for ADA. In the placebo group, 1/15 (7%) subjects tested positive for ADA post-baseline; in this subject, ADA-positive status was transient and non-neutralizing in nature.
L1709: ## **PD Results:**
L1710: Administration of tezepelumab to subjects with mild atopic asthma attenuated the late asthmatic response (between 3 and 7 hours post allergen inhalation challenge) and early asthmatic response (between 0 and 2 hours post allergen inhalation challenge), as measured by AUC for the maximum percent fall in FEV1 on Days 42 and 84 following the allergen challenge. In addition, tezepelumab suppressed the inhaled allergen-induced increase in blood and sputum eosinophils and FeNO relative to placebo.
L1711: ## **16.2.2.2. Phase 2 Studies**
L1712: - **Study D5180C00013 (CASCADE)**
L1713: **Study Type:** Phase 2 efficacy, and safety trial in adult subjects with inadequately controlled asthma on ICS and at least one additional asthma controller
L1714: **Title:** A Phase 2, Randomized, Double-blind, Parallel Group, Placebo Controlled Study to Evaluate the Effect of Tezepelumab on Airway Inflammation in Adults with Inadequately Controlled Asthma on Inhaled Corticosteroids and at Least One Additional Asthma Controller
L1715: ## **Objective:**
L1716: The primary objective of the study was to explore the airway anti-inflammatory effect of tezepelumab.
L1717: Secondary and exploratory objectives included the following:
L1718: - To evaluate the PK, PD, and immunogenicity of tezepelumab.
L1719: ## Study Design:
L1720: Subjects had a background asthma therapy of medium- or high-dose ICSs plus at least one additional asthma controller medication, with or without maintenance OCS. Subjects were randomised 1:1 to receive tezepelumab 210 mg Q4W or placebo, and entered a 4-week screening/run-in period, followed by a 28-week treatment period (with an optional 4-week to 24-week extension because of the coronavirus disease 2019 [COVID-19] pandemic), and a 12-week follow-up period. Pharmacokinetic sampling was conducted on Day 1 and Weeks 12, 28/EOT, and at Week 40/the end of follow-up (all pre-dose samples, except Weeks 28/EOT and 40/end of follow-up).
L1721: ## PK Results:
L1722: A total of 59 subjects were treated with tezepelumab and had evaluable PK data. The mean serum trough concentrations of tezepelumab were similar at Week 12 (22.5 µg/mL) and EOT (24.5 µg/mL).
L1723: ## Immunogenicity:
L1724: No (0/59) subjects on tezepelumab and 2/55 (3.6%) subjects on placebo tested positive for treatment-emergent ADAs.
L1725: ## PD Results:
L1726: The primary outcome variable of the study was change from baseline to EOT, expressed as a ratio, in the number of airway submucosal inflammatory cells/mm2 (eosinophils, neutrophils, T cells, and mast cells) from bronchial biopsies.
L1727: Tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W reduced airway inflammation over the course of treatment compared with placebo, as demonstrated by an 89% reduction in bronchial submucosal eosinophils (ratio for change from baseline to EOT of 0.11; 90% CI 0.06 to 0.21) in the tezepelumab group compared with a 25% reduction (ratio for change from baseline to EOT of 0.75; 90% CI 0.41 to 1.38) in the placebo group. This equated to a 6.7-fold reduction in bronchial submucosal eosinophils from baseline to EOT compared with placebo (ratio of geometric least squares mean of 0.15; 90% CI 0.06 to 0.35). Tezepelumab had no detectable effect on bronchial submucosal neutrophil, CD3+ and CD4+ T-cell, or tryptase+ and chymase+ mast cells counts, RBM thickness, or % airway epithelial integrity.
L1728: The decrease in bronchial submucosal eosinophils was seen irrespective of baseline levels of blood eosinophils, FeNO, total serum IgE, allergic/non-allergic status (defined by a positive serum IgE result specific to a panel of perennial aeroallergens in the fluorescent enzyme immunoassay [FEIA] versus negative results for all perennial aeroallergens in the panel), serum IL-5, and serum IL-13. Treatment with tezepelumab also resulted in reduced levels of blood eosinophils, FeNO, total serum IgE, serum IL-5, serum IL-13, and plasma EDN (an eosinophil activation marker).
L1729: - Study CD-RIMEDI9929-1146 (PATHWAY)
L1730: NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {BLA 761224}
L1731: {Tezspire/Tezepelumab}
L1732: **Study Type:** Phase 2b efficacy, and safety trial in adult subjects with severe, inadequately controlled asthma
L1733: **Title:** A Phase 2 Randomized, Double-blind, Placebo-controlled Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of MEDI9929 in Adult Subjects with Inadequately Controlled, Severe Asthma
L1734: ## **Objective:**
L1735: The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the effect of 3 dose levels of tezepelumab on asthma exacerbations in adult subjects with inadequately controlled, severe asthma.
L1736: Secondary and exploratory objectives included the following:
L1737: - To determine the optimal dose and regimen of tezepelumab to be used in later studies.
- To describe the PK and immunogenicity of tezepelumab.
- To assess the PD effect of tezepelumab on biomarkers.
L1738: ## **Study Design:**
L1739: The study recruited adult subjects with inadequately controlled severe asthma on medium-dose or high-dose inhaled corticosteroids (ICS) and at least one additional controller. Subjects were randomised 1:1:1:1 to receive one of 3 dose levels of SC Tezepelumab (70 mg Q4W, 210 mg Q4W, or 280 mg Q2W) or placebo Q2W, and entered a 5-week screening/run-in period, followed by a 52-week treatment period, and a 12-week follow-up period. Pharmacokinetic sampling was conducted on Day 1 and Weeks 4, 12, 20, 28, 40, 52, and 64 (all pre-dose samples, except for Weeks 52 and 64).
L1740: Thirty-four subjects from one site in the United States were excluded from all populations (including the evaluable for PK population) due to suspected non-compliance with Good Clinical Practice. Exclusion of the 34 subjects did not alter study conclusions.
L1741: ## **PK Results:**
L1742: A total of 550 subjects randomised (138, 137, 137, and 138 subjects in the 70 mg Q4W tezepelumab, 210 mg Q4W tezepelumab, 280 mg Q2W tezepelumab, and placebo groups, respectively) were included in analyses. Pharmacokinetic data were evaluable in a total of 393 subjects who received tezepelumab (133, 128, and 132 subjects in the 70 mg Q4W, 210 mg Q4W, and 280 mg Q2W groups, respectively).
L1743: After repeated SC administration of tezepelumab 70 mg Q4W, 210 mg Q4W, and 280 mg Q2W, the mean serum trough concentration increased over time and achieved steady state by Week 12. Tezepelumab exhibited linear PK across the 3 doses. The mean accumulation ratios were 1.65 to 1.8, based on ratio between the trough concentrations at Week 52 and Week 4.
L1744: ## **Immunogenicity:**
L1745: Thirteen out of 138 (9.4%), 5/137 (3.6%), 1/132 (0.8%), and 3/133 (2.3%) subjects tested positive for ADA post-baseline in the placebo, 70 mg Q4W, 210 mg Q4W, and 280 mg Q2W groups, respectively. There were no nAbs detected for the ADA-positive samples. The presence of ADA did not appear to impact tezepelumab PK in the subjects with ADA.
L1746: ## **PD Results:**
L1747: In exploratory biomarker analyses, treatment with tezepelumab resulted in substantial and persistent reductions in the levels of peripheral blood eosinophils and FeNO and a progressive
L1748: decrease in total serum IgE levels. The reductions in blood eosinophils and FeNO were seen as early as Week 4 (first time point assessed) and were maintained throughout the 52-week treatment period.
L1749: ## 16.2.2.3. Phase 3 Studies
L1750: ## - Study D5180C00007 (NAVIGATOR)
L1751: **Study Type:** Phase 3 efficacy and safety trial in adult and adolescent subjects with severe uncontrolled asthma
L1752: **Title:** A Multicenter, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo Controlled, Parallel Group, Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Tezepelumab in Adults and Adolescents with Severe Uncontrolled Asthma
L1753: ## **Objective:**
L1754: The primary objective of the study was to assess the effect of 210 mg tezepelumab SC Q4W on asthma exacerbations in adult and adolescent subjects with severe uncontrolled asthma compared with placebo.
L1755: Secondary and exploratory objectives included the following:
L1756: - To evaluate the PK and immunogenicity of tezepelumab.
- To assess the PD effect of 210 mg of tezepelumab SC Q4W on biomarkers.
L1757: ## **Study Design:**
L1758: The study recruited adult (\ge 18 years of age) and adolescent subjects (\ge 12 to &lt; 18 years of age) with a history of asthma exacerbations and severe asthma receiving medium- or high-dose ICS plus at least one additional asthma controller medication with or without OCS. Subjects were randomised to tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W or placebo for up to 52 weeks. Pharmacokinetic sampling was conducted on Day 1 and Weeks 4, 12, 24, 36, 52, and 64 (all pre-dose samples, except for Weeks 52 and 64).
L1759: ## **PK Results:**
L1760: The PK analysis set contained a total of 526 subjects who were treated with tezepelumab and had at least one PK sample post first dose with quantifiable serum concentrations of tezepelumab. After administration of tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W, the mean serum trough concentration increased over time, approaching steady state by Week 12. At Weeks 4, 12, 24, 36, and 52 (EOT), mean serum trough concentrations were 11.5, 20.7, 22.5, 21.9, and 22.6 \mu\text{g/mL}, respectively. The mean serum trough concentrations were higher in adolescent subjects (age 12 to &lt; 18 years) than in adults, due to the lower average body weight in the adolescents, and similar between adults of age 18 to &lt; 65 years and those \ge 65 years of age. At Week 52, the mean serum trough concentrations were 27.9, 21.8, and 23.8 \mu\text{g/mL} in adolescents, adults 18 to &lt; 65 years and \ge 65 years of age, respectively.
L1761: ## **Immunogenicity:**
L1762: The incidence (ie, the proportion of subjects who were treatment emergent ADA positive) of ADAs was low. In the overall population, 10/522 (1.9%) subjects on tezepelumab and 20/523 (3.8%) subjects on placebo tested positive for treatment-emergent ADAs. One subject on tezepelumab and one subject on placebo had nAbs. In total, 26 subjects treated with tezepelumab
L1763: were ADA positive at any time, of whom 15 were ADA positive at baseline only, 7 were transiently positive, and 4 were classified as persistently positive. In both the tezepelumab and placebo treatment groups, the median of the maximum ADA titers were low (67.20 in the tezepelumab group and 134.40 in the placebo group; the limit of detection for the ADA assay was 67.20) throughout the course of the study. The median of the maximum ADA titre of the treatment-emergent ADA positive subjects was 100.80 in each treatment group. No adolescent subjects in either treatment group tested positive for treatment-emergent ADAs or nAbs. There was no apparent effect of ADA on PK, PD, efficacy, or safety.
L1764: ## **PD Results:**
L1765: Tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W reduced biomarkers and cytokines associated with inflammation from baseline versus placebo, with an onset of effect by 2 weeks and sustained reduction to 52 weeks for blood eosinophil counts, FeNO, serum IL-5, and serum IL-13. Tezepelumab also caused a progressive reduction in total serum IgE, with levels continuing to decrease throughout 52 weeks of treatment.
L1766: - Study D5180C00009 (SOURCE)
L1767: **Study Type:** Phase 3 efficacy and safety trial in adult subjects with OCS-dependent asthma
L1768: **Title:** A Multicenter, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo Controlled, Phase 3 Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Tezepelumab in Reducing Oral Corticosteroid Use in Adults with Oral Corticosteroid Dependent Asthma
L1769: ## **Objective:**
L1770: The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the effect of Tezepelumab compared with placebo in reducing the prescribed OCS maintenance dose in subjects with asthma requiring chronic treatment with maintenance OCS in addition to high-dose ICS plus a long-acting \beta2 agonist (LABA).
L1771: Secondary and exploratory objectives included the following:
L1772: - To evaluate the PK and immunogenicity of tezepelumab
- To assess the PD effect of tezepelumab on biomarkers.
L1773: ## **Study Design:**
L1774: The study recruited adult subjects with asthma requiring treatment with maintenance OCS in combination with ICS + a LABA with or without other asthma controller therapy. Subjects were randomised to tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W or placebo and entered a 2-week screening/run-in period, followed by an up to 8-week OCS optimization phase, a 48-week treatment period (including an OCS-reduction phase), and a 12-week follow-up period. Pharmacokinetic sampling was conducted on Day 1 and Weeks 4, 12, 24, 40, 48, and 60 (all pre-dose samples, except Weeks 48 and 60).
L1775: ## **PK Results:**
L1776: The PK analysis set contained a total of 73 subjects who were treated with tezepelumab and provided at least one PK sample post first dose that contained quantifiable serum concentrations of tezepelumab. After administration of tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W, the mean serum trough
L1777: concentration increased over time, approaching steady state by Week 12. At Weeks 4, 12, 24, 40, and 48, the mean serum trough concentrations of tezepelumab were 11.2, 20.0, 21.3, 21.8, and 20.2 µg/mL, respectively.
L1778: ## **Immunogenicity:**
L1779: The incidence of ADAs was low; 1/73 (1.4%) subjects on tezepelumab and 0/76 (0%) subjects on placebo tested positive for treatment-emergent ADAs. No subjects in either treatment group tested positive for nAbs. Anti-drug antibodies had no apparent effect on PK or safety.
L1780: ## **PD Results:**
L1781: Tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W reduced biomarkers and cytokines associated with inflammation from baseline versus placebo with an onset of effect by 4 weeks and sustained reduction to 48 weeks for blood eosinophil counts, FeNO, serum IL-5, and serum IL-13. Tezepelumab caused a progressive reduction in total serum IgE, with levels continuing to decrease throughout 48 weeks of treatment.
L1782: - Study D5180C00011 (PATH-HOME)
L1783: **Study Type:** Phase 3 open label device functionality study (APFS &amp; AI) in adult and adolescent subjects with severe asthma
L1784: **Title:** A Multicenter, Randomized, Open-label, Parallel-group, Functionality, and Performance Study of an Accessorized Pre-filled Syringe and Autoinjector with Home-administered Subcutaneous Tezepelumab in Adolescent and Adult Subjects with Severe Asthma
L1785: ## **Objective:**
L1786: The primary objective of the study was to assess the successful administration of tezepelumab 210 mg SC by injection with an APFS or AI in the clinic and at home.
L1787: Secondary and exploratory objectives included the following:
L1788: - To assess the functionality and performance of APFS and AI presentations.
- To assess the PK and immunogenicity of tezepelumab administered via APFS or AI in the clinic and at home.
L1789: ## **Study Design:**
L1790: The study recruited adult and adolescent subjects with severe asthma. All subjects received tezepelumab 210 mg SC Q4W delivered using the APFS or AI and entered a 2-week screening/run-in period, followed by a 24-week treatment period and a 12-week follow-up period. Pharmacokinetic sampling was conducted on Day 1 (pre-dose) and Weeks 4 (pre-dose), 20 (pre-dose), 24, and 36.
L1791: ## **PK Results:**
L1792: The PK analysis set contained a total of 216 subjects (111 from the APFS group and 105 from the AI group) who were treated with tezepelumab and had at least one post-treatment sample with quantifiable serum concentrations of tezepelumab. At all the post-dose time points the mean serum concentrations of tezepelumab were similar between the APFS and the AI groups. At Week 4, Week 20, Week 24 (EOT), and Week 36 (follow-up) the mean serum concentrations of tezepelumab were 12.0, 21.9, 22.2, and 2.65 µg/mL, respectively, in the APFS group, and 11.8, 23.0, 22.3, and 2.92 µg/mL, respectively, in the AI group.
L1793: ## **Immunogenicity:**
L1794: The incidence of ADAs was low following treatment with tezepelumab, with 1.8% (2/111) of subjects in the APFS group and 7.6% (8/105) of subjects in the AI group testing positive for treatment-emergent ADA. Overall, ADA titers to tezepelumab were low in both the APFS and AI groups. The median of the maximum ADA titre of the post-dose ADA-positive samples achieved in any subject through study completion was 67.20 (limit of detection of the ADA assay) both for subjects using the APFS device and for subjects using the AI device. The subject with the highest maximum titre (537.60) in the study was ADA positive at baseline only.

L1803: ## 1. EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
L1804: This Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) review is provided as a response to a request for consultation by the Division of Pulmonology, Allergy, and Critical Care (DPACC) regarding BLA 761224 for tezepelumab. The applicant has submitted an original application for tezepelumab indicated for the add-on maintenance treatment of patients aged 12 years and older with (b)(4) severe asthma (b)(4). This indication is being supported by three cores studies1, 2, 3: two exacerbation studies (NAVIGATOR and PATHWAY) and one oral corticosteroid (OCS)-reduction study (SOURCE).
L1805: The applicant proposes (b)(4) the following ranked secondary efficacy patient-reported outcome (PRO) endpoints in the NAVIGATOR Study:
L1806: - Change from baseline in Standardized Asthma Quality of Life Questionnaire for 12 years and older (AQLQ(s) +12) total score at Week 52
- Change from baseline in Asthma Control Questionnaire-6 (ACQ-6) Score at Week 52
- Change from baseline in weekly mean daily Asthma Symptom Diary (ASD) score at Week 52
L1807: Data from the NAVIGATOR Study demonstrated that tezepelumab treatment resulted in statistically significant improvement in the AQLQ, ACQ, and ASD scores compared with placebo. The AQLQ and ACQ have regulatory precedent for labeling. As such, (b)(4)
L1808: the applicant submitted a COA evidence dossier for review.
L1809: The review concludes that the evidence submitted by the applicant is sufficient to demonstrate that the ASD is fit-for-purpose4 to measure asthma symptom severity for the context of use of this drug development program. (b)(4)
L1810: 1 NAVIGATOR Study: A phase 3, multicenter, multinational, randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blind, pivotal clinical trial conducted in adults and adolescents aged 12 to 80 years with inadequately controlled asthma with a 52-week treatment period (N = 1061 randomized and 1059 treated).
L1811: 2 PATHWAY Study: A phase 2b, dose-ranging, asthma exacerbation study in adults aged 18 to 75 years with a 52-week treatment period (N = 550 randomized and treated).
L1812: 3 SOURCE Study: A phase 3 OCS-reduction study in adults aged 18 to 80 years with OCS-dependent asthma with a 48-week treatment period (N = 150 randomized and treated).
L1813: 4 Fit-for-purpose: A conclusion that the level of validation associated with a tool is sufficient to support its context of use. (Source: BEST (Biomarkers, Endpoints and Other Tools) Resource; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK338448/)
L1814: ## 2. REVIEW CONCLUSIONS
L1815: The ASD was reviewed for content validity, quantitative measurement properties, and the applicant's proposed threshold for meaningful within-patient score change. The review concludes the following:
L1816: - The evidence submitted by the applicant is sufficient to demonstrate that the ASD is fit-for-purpose5 to measure asthma symptom severity for the context of use of this drug development program.
- While the change in ASD at Week 52 showed a modest statistically significant between-group difference, as supported by a small separation in the empirical cumulative distribution function (eCDF) curves for tezepelumab vs. placebo, there is lack of information supporting the interpretability and clinical meaningfulness of the statistically significant results. The applicant is limited in their ability to provide supportive evidence for interpretability and clinical meaningfulness of the ASD score given that appropriate anchor scales that would be useful for deriving meaningful change thresholds were not administered in the NAVIAGTOR study. (b) (4)
- For future clinical trials in patients with asthma, we recommend that appropriate anchor scales (i.e., easy-to-interpret global scales that measure similar concepts and have the same recall period as the target ASD endpoint) be included to facilitate interpretation of clinically meaningful within-patient changes in ASD scores from the patient perspective. (b) (4)
L1817: ## 3 BACKGROUND AND CORRESPONDENCE ON COA
L1818: ## Regulatory Background:
L1819: FDA informed the applicant in a Type B End-of-Phase 2 Meeting Preliminary Comments dated July 3, 2017 (Reference ID: 4119840) and Meeting Minutes dated November 13, 2017 (Reference ID: 4180617) that the use of the Asthma Symptom Diary (ASD) in the phase 3 trials is at their discretion and should be supported with a rationale for its inclusion (b) (4)
L1820: In addition, FDA recommended evaluation of patients using Asthma Control Questionnaire-5 (ACQ-5) and ACQ-7 to assess asthma control and the associated asthma symptoms, and the Asthma Quality of Life Questionnaire (AQLQ) to describe the degree to which these symptoms affect quality of life (see Appendices for copies of the ACQ and AQLQ). FDA stated that these endpoints are clinically meaningful (b) (4)
L1821: 5 Fit-for-purpose: A conclusion that the level of validation associated with a tool is sufficient to support its context of use. (Source: BEST (Biomarkers, Endpoints and Other Tools) Resource; https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK338448/)
L1822: (b) (4) presented as a responder analysis (in which a responder is defined as achieving an improvement in score of 0.5 or more as threshold), regardless of the results or statistical significance. FDA also stated that there is precedent for inclusion of the ACQ and AQLQ, presented as a responder analysis, in Section 14 of product labels (e.g., Nucala and Cinqair).
L1823: ## **Previous COA Reviews:**
L1824: There are no previous COA reviews for IND 103031.
L1825: ## **Disease Background:**
L1826: Asthma is a common chronic heterogeneous inflammatory airway disease caused by the interaction of genetic and environmental factors. It is characterized by bronchial hyperresponsiveness, variable symptoms of wheezing, breathlessness, chest tightness and cough, and by variable expiratory airflow obstruction. Asthma may also be characterized by exacerbations, which are episodes of worsening symptoms and lung function. Asthma constitutes major public health consequences for both children and adults, including high morbidity, and mortality in severe cases.
L1827: ## **Investigational Product:**
L1828: Tezepelumab is an anti-thymic stromal lymphopoietin (TSLP), human monoclonal antibody (IgG2λ) that blocks human TSLP, an epithelial cell-derived cytokine, that occupies an upstream position in the asthma inflammatory cascade and plays a central role in the initiation and persistence of airway inflammation in asthma.
L1829: ## **Materials reviewed:**
L1830: - ASD Dossier
- Response to COA Information Request dated September 17, 2021 (SN 0021)
- Clinical Study Protocol and Protocol Amendments (SN 0001)
- Clinical Study Report (SN 0001)
L1831: ## 4 CLINICAL OUTCOME ASSESSMENT REVIEW
L1832: ## 4.1 Clinical Trial Design
L1833: The NAVIGATOR study was a global, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo controlled, parallel group, phase 3 study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of 210 mg every 4 week (subcutaneous) of tezepelumab in adolescents and adults with severe uncontrolled asthma.
L1834: The study consisted of a screening/run-in period between 5-6 weeks, a treatment period of 52 weeks and a post-treatment follow-up period of 12 weeks.
L1835: ## 4.2 Clinical Trial Population
L1836: The target population for the NAVIGATOR study are adolescents and adults aged 12 to 80 years with inadequately controlled asthma on medium to high dose inhaled corticosteroid and at least one additional asthma controller medication with or without oral corticosteroids.
L1837: A complete list of the inclusion and exclusion criteria is summarized in Section 5 of the Clinical Study Protocol for the NAVIGATOR study.
L1838: The study randomized 1061 subjects to either tezepelumab or placebo (1:1) globally from about 400 sites, and 1059 subjects were treated.
L1839: ## 4.3 Endpoint Position, Definition, and Assessment Schedule
L1840: Table 1 describes the placement of the COAs in the endpoint hierarchy, including the endpoint definition and assessment schedule for the NAVIGATOR study.

Weeks 2, 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, and 52 (EOT)

Key Secondary
☒ Multiplicity adjusted	Standardized Asthma Quality of Life Questionnaire for 12 years and older (AQLQ(s) +12)	Change from baseline in AQLQ(S)+12 total score at Week 52	Randomization (Week 0)
Weeks 4, 12, 24, 36, and 52 (EOT)

Key Secondary
☒ Multiplicity adjusted	Asthma Control Questionnaire-6 (ACQ-6)	Change from baseline in ACQ-6 Score at Week 52	Screening (Days -42 to -35)
Weeks 2, 4, and every 4 weeks thereafter until Week 52 (EOT)

Key Secondary
☒ Multiplicity adjusted	Asthma Symptom Diary (ASD)	Change from baseline in weekly mean daily ASD score at Week 52	Twice daily from Run-in to EOT
L1843: **Reviewer's comment(s):** The last measurement on or prior to the date of randomization served as the baseline measurement, with no imputation for missing values. For weekly means derived
L1844: from the ASD, baseline was defined as the mean of the available data in the most recent week prior to the first dose of study treatment. If more than 3 days were missing, then the baseline weekly mean was set to missing.
L1845: The primary and key secondary endpoints were controlled for Type I error using a hierarchical testing strategy as presented in Table 1, except for the AQLQ(S)+12 and ACQ-6 which were tested simultaneously. For the above-mentioned endpoints, change from baseline was defined as the absolute difference between the measurement at the Week 52 post-randomization time point and the baseline value. Difference in means at Week 52 were estimated using a repeated measures model with change from baseline at each week post randomization and up to and including Week 52 as response variable, and baseline, treatment, week, region, age and treatment by week interaction as covariates in the model.
L1846: The Patient Global Impression of Severity/Change (PGI-S/PGI-C) were also administered at Randomization (PGI-S only), Weeks 2, 4, 8, 12, and 52, and used as other secondary endpoint measures (i.e., proportion of responses at Week 52).
L1847: (b) (4)
L1848: ## 5 CLINICAL OUTCOME ASSESSMENT
L1849: ## 5.1.1 Clinical Outcome Assessment Description
L1850: ## Asthma Symptom Diary (ASD)
L1851: The ASD (see Appendix A) is a twice-daily diary that assesses the severity of asthma symptoms and comprises 10 items (5 items in the morning; 5 items in the evening). The morning items assess nighttime symptom severity in relation to wheezing, shortness of breath, cough, and chest tightness, and the frequency of nighttime awakenings. The evening items assess symptom severity in relation to wheezing, shortness of breath, cough, and chest tightness, and activity limitation since waking. Respondents answer each question on a five-point scale, from zero (not at all) to four (very severe) and completed the ASD twice daily at home on an electronic handheld device.
L1852: ## 5.1.2 Conceptual Framework
L1853: The conceptual framework of the ASD is shown in Figure 1.
L1854: **Figure 1.** Conceptual Framework of the ASD
L1855: ## 5.1.3 Scoring Algorithm
L1856: ASD items are scored from “0” (no symptom, no nighttime awakening, or no activity limitation) to “4” (very severe symptom, unable to sleep, or extreme activity limitation). A daily ASD score is the mean of the 10 items. Responses for all 10 items are required to calculate the daily ASD score; otherwise, it is treated as missing. Calculation of a daily ASD score requires data from the evening diary assessment and the subsequent morning diary assessment. For the 7-day average asthma symptom score, scoring is done with no imputation using the mean of at least 4 of the 7 daily ASD scores as a mean weekly item score. The 7-day average ASD score ranges from 0 – 4.
L1857: ## 5.1.4 Content Validity
L1858: Evidence of content validity of the ASD was derived from:
L1859: - Literature review
- Clinical expert input to confirm the clinical relevance of the concepts reported by asthma patients and captured in the ASD
- Patient input through (1) qualitative concept elicitation interviews with adults [n=34] and adolescents [\ge12 to 17 years old, n=16], to obtain evidence supporting concept relevance and saturation of concepts, and (2) three rounds of cognitive interviews with 24 additional adult and adolescent asthma patients to further support the saturation and relevancy of items, and to refine each measure to ensure clarity and understanding among the target patient population. See Table 3 and Table 4 for the demographic and clinical characteristics of the adolescent and adult subjects who participated in the concept elicitation and cognitive interviews.
- Lexibility and translatability assessment to ensure suitable reading level for the general population
- Assessment of electronic usability to confirm the suitability and ease of use of the ASD on the electronic device

L1865: ACQ = Asthma Control Questionnaire; FEV1 = forced expiratory volume in 1 second; SD = standard deviation
L1866: **Reviewer's comment(s):** The sample for the qualitative research appears appropriate. Based on the results of the concept elicitation interviews, the most common symptoms of asthma reported by patients as most important were shortness of breath, chest tightness (most commonly expressed by adults), coughing, and wheezing (most commonly expressed by adolescents), and the occurrence/severity of these symptoms were reported to vary between day and night. Patients also reported that the most important attribute of their symptom experience (severity, frequency, or duration) to measure was severity. Activity limitations and sleep disturbances were identified as markers of symptom severity based on the qualitative research (patient spontaneous self-report) and the literature. The results of the cognitive interviews showed that the instructions, recall period, items and response options of the ASD were clear and appropriate from the patients' perspective, which was confirmed in the usability study. The ASD is culturally adapted and translated and is available in 18 languages.
L1867: Overall, the applicant has provided sufficient evidence to support the content validity of the ASD.
L1868: (b) (4)
L1869: ## 5.1.5 Quantitative Measurement Properties
L1870: To assess the quantitative measurement properties of the ASD, the applicant used data from a multicenter, prospective, observational study, in which there were 276 asthma participants (250 adults and 26 adolescents; mean age [SD] = 42.9 [16.4]; 70% female [n=192]; 70% White [n=195]; mean asthma duration [SD] = 23.3 [16.8] years) who completed the ASD twice daily for 28 days. Participants also completed the first 6 items of the ACQ, AQLQ12+, and the Work Productivity and Activity Impairment Questionnaire: Asthma (WPAI-Asthma; subjects aged 16 and older only) at each study visit, and the Patient Global Impression of Change (PGIC) at Visit 2 and Visit 3.
L1871: Data were analyzed as follows:
L1872: - Item pool analyses (means, standard deviation, floor effect, ceiling effect, percentage of missing, and inter-item correlation) were performed on baseline values to identify poorly performing items as candidates for deletion.
- Factor and Rasch analyses were conducted to further support content validity, explore dimensionality, and test for measurement gaps.
- The ASD's measurement properties were analyzed, assessing internal consistency and test-retest reliability, construct and known-groups validity, and ability to detect change.
L1873: Results from these analyses showed:
L1874: - No notable ceiling effects (maximum response &gt;25%) were obtained for the ASD items at Visit 1; however, there was a large daily floor effect (minimum response &gt;25%) for these items ranging from 39.3% to 67.7% of the sample. Weekly floor effect (defined as selecting the "No Symptom" responses for 7 consecutive days) from Day 2 to Day 8 for each item ranged from 23.1% to 44.9%.
- The one-dimensional model fitted to the ASD items for the confirmatory factor analysis had the following fit statistics: confirmatory fit index (CFI) = 0.952, root mean square error of approximation (RMSEA) = 0.179, and weighted root mean square residual (WRMR) = 1.415.
L1875: No items were identified as mis-fitting the Rasch model as indicated by no significant chi-square results (p &gt; 0.05); in addition, the items displayed fit residuals within the \pm 2.5 range (indicating optimal fit), except for one item which exhibited a fit residual of 2.641.
L1876: No item exhibiting DIF for gender based on a p-value of 0.005 adjusting for multiple comparisons using Bonferroni correction. No DIF test was conducted for age due to the small number of adolescents in the study.
L1877: - A high internal reliability consistency (Cronbach's alpha = 0.94)
- A strong test-retest reliability (intra-class correlation coefficient [ICC] range: 0.89-0.95; concordance correlation coefficient [CCC] range 0.89-0.95) between Visit 1 and Visit 2 ASD 7-day average score among all 3 stable groups, each respectively defined by PGIC ('no change') at Visit 2 as the primary analysis, and by FEV1 change (&lt;8%) and ACQ change (&lt;0.5) at Visit 2 as the secondary/confirmatory analysis.
- A statistically significant strong correlations (r &gt; 0.50, p &lt; 0.001) between the ASD 7-Day average score and the ACQ, AQLQ12+, WPAI-Asthma (except absenteeism where r=0.36), thus demonstrating strong convergent validity.
L1878: A statistically significant (p &lt; 0.0001) pair-wise comparisons for the ASD 7-day average asthma symptom score across all known-group definitions: clinician rating of asthma control ("well-controlled"; "not-well-controlled"; and "very-poorly- controlled" asthma), ACQ score (\le 0.75; &gt; 0.75 to &lt; 1.5; and \ge 1.5), and AQLQ12+ symptom score (above and below the median).
L1879: - The ability of the ASD 7-day average score to detect change as indicated by significantly higher (p &lt; 0.0001) change scores at Visit 3 for the responder group than non-responder group as defined by all 3 criteria measures (asthma control, ACQ score, and PGIC).
L1880: **Reviewer's comment(s):** The floor effect may be explained by the fluctuation in the occurrence of asthma symptoms; more so, the qualitative data do support all items to be clinically relevant. Overall, the ASD demonstrated acceptable psychometric properties. Of note, the dimensionality of the ASD is questionable given the fit statistics for the confirmatory factor analysis did not meet the thresholds for acceptable fit (i.e., CFI \ge 0.95, RMSEA \le 0.08, WRMR &lt; 1) and one item exhibited a high positive residual of 2.641 which may suggest multi-dimensionality. Future validation work should confirm the dimensionality of the ASD.
L1881: ## 5.1.6 Interpretation of Meaningful Within-Patient Score Changes
L1882: The applicant proposed that a \ge 0.5-point reduction (on a 0 to 4 scale) is a meaningful within-patient score change in the 7-day average ASD score based on a secondary anchor-based analysis of ASD data collected daily during their 24-week phase II (NCT01902290, testing brodalumab) clinical trial in patients with moderate to severe asthma. Patients obtaining change scores less than or equal to the threshold (-0.50) were designated as responders; patients obtaining greater than the threshold were designated as non-responders. The responder/non-responder were defined by clinician rating of asthma control (better or above versus others), by PGIC
L1883: classification (a little better or above versus others), and by change in ACQ score (decrease \ge 0.5 versus others) at Week 12 (from Baseline to Week 12), and then at Week 24 (from Week 12 to Week 24).
L1884: **Reviewer's comment(s):** The interpretability of the anchor-based analyses would be difficult as the anchor scales used in the analyses had limitations [e.g., the anchor scales were not easy to interpret (i.e., the ACQ), did not measure a concept similar to the ASD from the patient's perspective (i.e., the clinician rating of asthma control), or were prone to recall bias (i.e., PGIC)]. In the applicant's response to the COA Information Request dated September 17, 2021 (SDN 0021), copies of the PGIS and PGIC anchor scales included in the NAVIGATOR study (Appendix B and C) showed that the recall period did not match the target ASD endpoint, and the ASD 7-day mean score correlations with the PGIS and PGIC scores at Week 52 were not strong [0.66 (0.51 at baseline) and 0.45, respectively], thus limiting the utility of these scales as anchors. Further confirmatory anchor-based analyses will need to be conducted in the future to facilitate the interpretability of the clinical meaningfulness of the ASD change scores.

L1965: **SUBJECT:** Pharmacology/Toxicology Secondary Review
L1966: **APPLICATION/DRUG:** BLA 761224/ TEZSPIRE (Tezepelumab-ekko)
L1967: AstraZeneca submitted BLA 761224 via the 351(a) pathway to develop Tezepelumab-ekko subcutaneous injection for the add-on maintenance treatment of adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older with severe asthma. Tezepelumab-ekko is a human monoclonal antibody IgG2 lambda, and the Established Pharmacologic Class (EPC) is thymic stromal lymphopoietin (TSLP) blocker. This is the first product in this pharmacologic class. The proposed dosing regimen is 210 mg once every four weeks.
L1968: A full nonclinical program was conducted for Tezepelumab-ekko, including pharmacology studies to characterize the mechanism of action and to support the EPC, general toxicology studies in monkeys to characterize the toxicity profile, and reproductive and developmental toxicity studies in monkeys. A carcinogenicity risk assessment was submitted by the Applicant and reviewed by the Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee. It was determined that a carcinogenicity study was not needed for Tezepelumab-ekko.
L1969: The nonclinical primary reviewer, Dr. Anup Srivastava, proposed some edits to the product labeling for the following sections: Indications and Usage (only the EPC), Section 8 (8.1 Pregnancy and 8.2 Lactation), Section 12.1 (Mechanism of Action), and Section 13 (Nonclinical Toxicology). I concur with Dr. Srivastava's proposed edits as captured in his review dated December 7th, 2021.
L1970: I concur with Dr. Srivastava's conclusion that the nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology
L1971: profiles of Tezepelumab have been adequately characterized and the nonclinical program is complete. The nonclinical recommendation is approval. Refer to the Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation for additional details.

L1981: ## 1 Executive Summary
L1982: ## 1.1 Introduction
L1983: AstraZeneca submitted a 351(a) BLA 761224 application on May 7, 2021 for the use of Tezepelumab (TEZSPIRE) subcutaneous injection for the add-on maintenance treatment of adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older with severe asthma. The BLA was filed on July 6, 2021 as a priority review with a PDUFA goal date of January 7, 2022.
L1984: Tezepelumab drug product for subcutaneous (SC) injection is a sterile, single-dose, preservative-free solution presented at 110 mg/mL concentration in a 1.91 mL deliverable volume in a vial and accessorized prefilled syringe (APFS). The recommended clinical dose is 210 mg tezepelumab to be administered once every four weeks.
L1985: This review evaluates the labeling content for TEZESPIRE (tezepelumab). Labeling recommendations were provided for Indications and Usage (under Highlights of Prescribing Information), Section 8.1 "Pregnancy", Section 8.2 "Lactation", Section 12.1 "Mechanism of Action", and Section 13 "Nonclinical Toxicology". Additions are denoted as underlined text. Deletions are denoted as strikeout text. The Applicant has agreed with the edits presented herein.
L1986: ## 1.3 Recommendations
L1987: ## 1.3.3 Final Labeling
L1988: ## ---------- INDICATIONS AND USAGE ----------
L1989: TEZSPIRE is a thymic stromal lymphopoietin (TSLP) blocker, human monoclonal antibody (IgG2\lambda), indicated for the add-on maintenance treatment of adult and pediatric patients aged 12 years and older with severe asthma.
L1990: ## 8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS
L1991: ## 8.1 Pregnancy
L1992: ## Risk Summary
L1993: There are no available data on TEZSPIRE use in pregnant women to evaluate for any drug-associated risk of major birth defects, miscarriage, or other adverse maternal or fetal outcomes. Placental transfer of monoclonal antibodies such as tezepelumab-ekko is greater during the third trimester of pregnancy; therefore, potential effects on a fetus are likely to be greater during the third trimester of pregnancy. In an enhanced pre- and post-natal development (ePPND) study conducted in cynomolgus monkeys, placental transport of tezepelumab-ekko was observed but there was no evidence of fetal harm
L1994: following intravenous administration of tezepelumab-ekko throughout pregnancy at doses that produced maternal exposures up to 168 times the exposure at the maximum recommended human dose (MRHD) of 210 mg administered subcutaneously [see Data].
L1995: The estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriages for the indicated population is unknown. All pregnancies have a background risk of birth defect, loss, or other adverse outcomes. In the U.S. general population, the estimated background risk of major birth defects and miscarriage in clinically recognized pregnancies is 2% to 4% and 15% to 20%, respectively.
L1996: ## Clinical Considerations
L1997: ## *Disease-associated maternal and/or embryo/fetal risk:*
L1998: In women with poorly or moderately controlled asthma, evidence demonstrates that there is an increased risk of preeclampsia in the mother and prematurity, low birth weight, and small for gestational age in the neonate. The level of asthma control should be closely monitored in pregnant women and treatment adjusted as necessary to maintain optimal control.
L1999: ## Data
L2000: ## *Animal Data*
L2001: In the ePPND study, pregnant cynomolgus monkeys received tezepelumab-ekko from GD20 to GD22 (dependent on pregnancy determination), at the beginning of organogenesis, and once every 7 days until the end of gestation at doses that produced exposures up to 168 times that achieved with the MRHD (on an AUC basis with maternal intravenous doses up to 300 mg/kg/week). There were no tezepelumab-ekko related adverse effects on maternal health, pregnancy outcome, embryo-fetal development, or neonatal growth and development up to 6.5 months of age. Tezepelumab-ekko crossed the placenta in cynomolgus monkeys and tezepelumab-ekko serum concentrations were 0.5- to 6.7-fold higher in infants relative to maternal animals.
L2002: ## **8.2 Lactation**
## Risk Summary
L2003: There is no information regarding the presence of tezepelumab-ekko in human milk, its effects on the breastfed infant, or its effects on milk production. However, tezepelumab-ekko is a human monoclonal antibody immunoglobulin G2\lambda (IgG2\lambda), and immunoglobulin G (IgG) is present in human milk in small amounts. Tezepelumab-ekko was present in the milk of cynomolgus monkeys postpartum following dosing during pregnancy [see Data]. The developmental and health benefits of breastfeeding should be considered along with the mother's clinical need for TEZSPIRE and any potential
L2004: adverse effects on the breastfed infant from TEZSPIRE or from the underlying maternal condition.
L2005: ## Data
L2006: ## *Animal Data*
L2007: In a prenatal and postnatal development study in cynomolgus monkeys, tezepelumab-ekko concentrations in milk were up to 0.5% of the maternal serum concentrations after intravenous administration of tezepelumab-ekko up to 300 mg/kg/week (168 times the exposures based on AUC achieved at MRHD). The concentration of tezepelumab-ekko in animal milk does not necessarily predict the concentration of drug in human milk.
L2008: ## 12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L2009: ## 12.1 Mechanism of Action
L2010: Tezepelumab-ekko is a thymic stromal lymphopoietin (TSLP) blocker, human monoclonal antibody IgG2λ that binds to human TSLP with a dissociation constant of 15.8 pM and blocks its interaction with the heterodimeric TSLP receptor. TSLP is a cytokine mainly derived from epithelial cells and occupies an upstream position in the asthma inflammatory cascade. TSLP may play multiple roles in the initiation and persistence of airway inflammation in asthma.
L2011: Airway inflammation is an important component in the pathogenesis of asthma. Multiple cell types (e.g., mast cells, eosinophils, neutrophils, macrophages, lymphocytes, ILC2 cells) and mediators (e.g., histamine, eicosanoids, leukotrienes, cytokines) are involved in airway inflammation. Blocking TSLP with tezepelumab-ekko reduces (b) (4) biomarkers and cytokines associated with inflammation including blood eosinophils, airway submucosal eosinophils, IgE, FeNO, IL-5, and IL-13; however, the mechanism of tezepelumab-ekko action in asthma has not been definitively established.
L2012: ## 13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
L2013: ## 13.1 Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, Impairment of Fertility
L2014: Animal studies have not been conducted to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of tezepelumab-ekko. The malignancy risk in humans from an antibody that blocks TSLP ligand, such as tezepelumab-ekko, is currently unknown.
L2015: Male and female fertility was unaffected based upon no observed adverse histopathological findings in the reproductive organs and no changes in menstrual cycle or semen analysis in sexually mature cynomolgus monkeys that received tezepelumab-ekko for 26 weeks at subcutaneous doses up to 300 mg/kg/week (approximately 134 times the MRHD on an AUC basis).
L2016: ## 11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation
L2017: Labeling recommendations were provided for Indications and Usage (under Highlights of Prescribing Information), Section 8.1 “Pregnancy”, Section 8.2 “Lactation”, Section 12.1 “Mechanism of Action”, and Section 13 “Nonclinical Toxicology”. Additions are denoted as underlined text. Deletions are denoted as strikeout text.

L2090: Flowchart illustrating the Multiplicity Testing procedure:
H01: AAER (all subjects): p \le 0.01
H02: AAER (subjects with baseline eosinophils < 300/\muL): p \le 0.05
H03: Pre-BD FEV_1 change from baseline at Week 52 (all subjects): p \le 0.05
H04a: AQLQ(S)+12 change from baseline at Week 52 (all subjects): p \le 0.0375 or p \le 0.025 (according to truncated Hochberg procedure)
H04b: ACQ-6 change from baseline at Week 52 (all subjects): p \le 0.025 or p \le 0.0375 (according to truncated Hochberg procedure)
H05: Weekly mean ASD score change from baseline at 52 weeks (all subjects): p \le 0.05 or p \le 0.0125 (according to truncated Hochberg outcome in previous step)

L2105: Flowchart illustrating the SOURCE Multiplicity strategy:
Test primary endpoint: cumulative odds ratio at 2-sided 5% significance.
Decision point: Is p < 0.05?If NO: Stop and no null hypothesis is rejected.
If YES: Proceed to Test key secondary endpoint: AAER ratio at 2-sided 5% significance.

L2112: Diagram illustrating the Population PK Model for Tezepelumab, a linear two-compartment model. The model consists of a Central compartment (C) and a Peripheral compartment (P). The Central compartment receives input from Intravenous (IV) administration and Subcutaneous (SC) administration (Absorption, A). The Central compartment is connected to the Peripheral compartment via inter-compartmental clearance (k_{cp} and k_{pc}). The Central compartment is also connected to elimination via k_e.
Legend:
k_a = absorption rate constant
k_e = elimination rate constant
k_{cp} = rate constant from central to peripheral
k_{pc} = rate constant from peripheral to central
F = bioavailability
CL = clearance
V_c = central volume
Q = inter-compartmental clearance
V_p = peripheral volume
IV = intravenous
SC = subcutaneous

The plots compare observed Tezepelumab Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) against predicted concentrations (IPRED or PRED) and Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) against Time (day) or PRED concentration.
Top Left Plot: Tezepelumab Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) vs IPRED (\mu\text{g/mL}). Both axes are logarithmic (0.01 to 1000). Data points (circles) are tightly clustered around the red solid line of equality.
Top Right Plot: Tezepelumab Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) vs PRED (\mu\text{g/mL}). Both axes are logarithmic (0.01 to 1000). Data points are tightly clustered around the red solid line of equality.
Bottom Left Plot: CWRES vs Time (day). The Y-axis (CWRES) ranges from -10 to 10. The X-axis (Time (day)) ranges from 0 to 600. The blue dashed line represents the LOWESS smooth curve, which is centered around 0, indicating good fit.
Bottom Right Plot: CWRES vs PRED (\mu\text{g/mL}). The Y-axis (CWRES) ranges from -10 to 10. The X-axis (PRED (\mu\text{g/mL})) ranges from 0.01 to 1000. The blue dashed line represents the LOWESS smooth curve, which is centered around 0, indicating good fit.

Top Right: Cavg,ss (\mug/mL). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 90. The median Cavg,ss decreases as body weight increases. A solid blue horizontal line indicates the mean Cavg,ss for 280 mg SC Q2W.
Bottom Left: Cmax,ss (\mug/mL). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 100. The median Cmax,ss decreases as body weight increases. A solid blue horizontal line indicates the mean Cmax,ss for 280 mg SC Q2W.
Bottom Right: Cmin,ss (\mug/mL). The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 80. The median Cmin,ss decreases as body weight increases. A solid blue horizontal line indicates the mean Cmin,ss for 280 mg SC Q2W.
In all plots, the solid black horizontal line within the box represents the median, the box represents the 25th to 75th percentiles (interquartile range), and the whiskers represent the range of data points within 1.5 times the interquartile range. The dashed red horizontal line represents the mean of the overall NAVIGATOR population.

The dotted vertical line at 1.0 represents no treatment difference. The plot indicates that Tezepelumab (Teze) consistently favors lower exacerbation rates compared to Placebo across all concentration quartiles.

The diagram illustrates the relationship between specific symptom items and the overarching concept of Asthma Symptoms.
Morning Items (Items 1-4) and Evening Items (Items 5-8) are listed on the left, each connected by an arrow to the central concept box labeled Asthma Symptoms.

L2126: Empirical Cumulative Distribution Function (ECDF) Curves showing the cumulative percentage of patients achieving a change from Baseline to Week 52 in Asthma Symptom Diary (ASD) score, stratified by Treatment Arm (Teze 210 mg Q4W vs. Placebo).
The X-axis represents the ASD Change from Baseline, ranging from -3.7 to 1.4. The Y-axis represents the Cumulative % of patients, ranging from 0 to 100.
A vertical dashed line is drawn at an ASD Change from Baseline of 0.2, indicating the threshold for "No Improvement". The region to the left of this line is labeled "Improvement" and the region to the right is labeled "No Improvement".
The Teze 210 mg Q4W group (blue line, circles) shows a higher cumulative percentage of patients achieving improvement compared to the Placebo group (red line, plus signs) across the measured range of ASD change.
Treatment Group: Teze 210 mg Q4W N=374 Median=-0.639, Placebo N=355 Median=-0.500.
